## Chapter 1

### 1: Introduction and Getting Started


https://youtu.be/uCTrQmNnpCM

# Lecture Notes: 

---

## Course Overview

This is the **second course** in the NEPQ Sales System. The session opens by contrasting how an **average salesperson** versus a **NEPQ-trained salesperson** would handle the same lead — calling someone who requested information from a digital marketing company's website.

---

## Key Concept: The Traditional Sales Approach (What NOT to Do)

### The Scenario
- A lead has requested information from a website selling digital marketing services
- The salesperson calls to follow up

### The Traditional Salesperson's Mistake
**Assumption:** Because the lead requested information, they must be highly interested and ready to buy right now.

### Example Script (Traditional Approach)
> *"Hey, this is Alex. I hope you're having a good day. My name is John Smith and you requested my company XYZ Digital Marketing to send you a packet of information on our marketing strategies that we offer to businesses like yours. Do you have two minutes to talk right now?"*

### What's Wrong With This Approach

1. **False assumption of buying intent** — Just because someone requests information doesn't mean they're ready to buy. Traditional sellers jump straight into pitch mode based on this flawed assumption.

2. **The "two minutes" trick** — Asking "do you have two minutes?" is a transparent tactic. The prospect knows it won't actually take two minutes. This immediately triggers skepticism and puts the prospect on guard.

3. **Prospect's internal reaction** — At this point, the prospect is already thinking about how to get off the call, not about how to engage. Trust is broken before the conversation even begins.

---

## The Core Insight

Traditional selling techniques create **resistance** from the very first interaction because:
- They rely on assumptions rather than understanding
- They use transparent tactics that prospects see through immediately
- They prioritize the salesperson's agenda over the prospect's actual needs

**NEPQ offers a fundamentally different model** — one focused on understanding the prospect's situation before attempting to sell anything.

---

## Takeaway for Agents

Before your next client call, ask yourself:
- Am I **assuming** interest, or have I **confirmed** it?
- Am I opening the conversation in a way that builds trust or triggers defense mechanisms?
- Would **I** want to stay on this call if I were the prospect?

---

*Next section will cover the NEPQ approach to the same scenario — demonstrating the contrast in effectiveness side by side.*

---

### 2: The Biggest Problem in Sales


https://youtu.be/-wW8aF7qM0g# 

---

## Introduction

The lecture opens by prompting salespeople to reflect on their current performance:

- Are you maximizing your income potential or commission plan?

This self-assessment sets the stage for identifying the core issue holding most salespeople back.

---

## The Biggest Problem in Sales

**The central problem: Salespeople fail to maximize their earning potential — not because of lack of opportunity, but because of how they approach the sales process itself.**

Key reflection questions for any sales professional:

1. Am I currently performing at my full income potential?
2. Am I fully leveraging my commission structure?
3. What gaps exist between where I am and where I could be?

---

## Key Takeaways

- **Self-awareness is the starting point.** Before learning new techniques, you must honestly assess your current performance level.
- **Most salespeople leave money on the table** — not from lack of leads or products, but from inefficiencies in their process, mindset, or approach.
- **Income potential vs. actual income** — understanding the gap between what's available to you and what you're actually earning is critical to growth.

---

## Application for Financial Advisors

- Regularly audit your own sales activity: appointment-to-close ratio, average case size, referral rate, and client retention.
- Identify whether the bottleneck is in **prospecting, presenting, closing, or follow-up** — the "biggest problem" is often different for each person.
- Use this reflection as a recurring exercise, not a one-time assessment.

---

### 3: My Lightbulb Moment

https://youtu.be/rzjdqvX-OKAHere's
---

## The Story: Early Struggles in Sales

The instructor shares his origin story as a broke college student whose first sales job was selling home security systems door-to-door.

**The setup:**
- Company gave him a script, dropped him and other reps in a neighborhood, and said "go make sales"
- Everyone in the office told him sales was easy
- Coming from a ranch in rural Missouri, he had no background in communication or selling

**What he did:**
- Followed the script exactly as given
- Was enthusiastic about features and benefits
- Focused on how much the product would help prospects

**What happened:**
- Constant objections: "We can't afford this," "We don't need it," "Your price is too high," "Let me think it over — call me back in a week/month/year"
- Despite his excitement, he was losing most prospects
- After 5–6 weeks of almost no sales, he was ready to quit

---

## The Turning Point: Tony Robbins Insight

Just before quitting, he heard Tony Robbins say something to the effect of:

> *"Most people fail for the simple reason they don't learn the right skills necessary to succeed."*

**The key distinction:** Everyone is taught skills — but the people who fail aren't learning the *right* ones.

**His lightbulb moment:**
- Maybe what his company and the "old sales gurus" were teaching him were simply outdated skills
- If those trainers never achieved the level of success he aspired to (seven figures), how could they teach him to get there?

---

## The Discovery: Behavioral Science Meets Sales

At the same time he was struggling in sales, he was studying **Behavioral Science and Human Psychology** in college, specializing in **Neuroscience** — the study of how the brain works, how people make decisions, and why people are persuaded (or not).

**The contradiction he noticed:**

| Traditional Sales Training | Behavioral Science |
|---|---|
| Push features and benefits | Get others to persuade themselves |
| Monologue / pitch-driven | Dialogue-driven |
| Tell people why they need the product | Ask Neuro-Emotional Persuasion Questions (NEPQs) |

The most persuasive way to sell — according to the science — was the **exact opposite** of what traditional sales training taught.

---

## Core Principles Established

1. **Enthusiasm alone doesn't close sales.** Being excited about your product doesn't overcome objections or create buying decisions.
2. **Scripts focused on features and benefits generate objections** — because they trigger resistance rather than self-discovery.
3. **The right skills matter more than effort.** Working hard with the wrong approach produces the same poor results.
4. **The most persuasive selling is dialogue, not monologue.** Asking the right questions lets prospects convince themselves.
5. **Question your teachers.** If your mentors never achieved the results you want, their methods may not get you there either.

---

## The Choice (Reflection for the Learner)

The lecture ends with a challenge:

- **Path A:** Stick to traditional selling techniques and get the same results you've always gotten
- **Path B:** Step outside your comfort zone, learn the most persuasive (science-backed) way to sell, and make more sales

---

### 5: Why Has the Consumer Changed


https://youtu.be/m9ipds2U4uM
# Lecture Notes: Why Has the Consumer Changed?

---

## The Core Shift: Technology Has Empowered the Consumer

The internet and social media have become the bridge between company and consumer, fundamentally changing the power dynamic in sales.

**Today's consumer already knows everything about you before you even speak to them:**

- Your company background and how long you've been in business
- Your products and services
- Your pricing
- Who your competitors are
- All discoverable through a simple Google search — even from their phone

---

## The Consequence: Consumers Will No Longer Be Pressured

Because consumers know they have **many other choices** for the exact same product or service you sell, pushy sales tactics no longer work. They have options, and they know it.

---

## Case Study: Buying a Used Car Today

A prospective Toyota Camry buyer can do all of the following **before ever stepping onto a dealership:**

1. **Find competing dealerships** offering the same car within a certain radius of their home
2. **Check dealer reputations** through social media networks and review websites to see if previous customers were happy
3. **Visit online forums** to learn what current Camry owners think of the car
4. **Research pricing** on Kelley Blue Book, Edmunds, or AutoTrader to know the fair market value
5. **Run the VIN number** to check for accidents or major repairs

The buyer arrives more informed than many salespeople expect — and this applies to virtually every industry, not just cars.

---

## The New Consumer Reality

Today's consumer is:

- **Wiser** — they research before engaging
- **More informed** — they know your product, pricing, and competitors
- **More prepared** — they come with data and comparisons
- **Less trusting** — they're skeptical of anything that comes out of a salesperson's mouth

---

## The Social Media Accountability Factor

If a consumer has a bad experience with you, they no longer just complain to a friend. They can:

- Tell hundreds of Facebook friends or Twitter followers
- Write about it on their blog
- Have their posts reshared across networks

This completely undermines the seller's ability to sell — one bad interaction can ripple across an entire community.

---

## The Bottom Line

Traditional selling techniques cause today's informed, empowered consumers to **run the other way**. Consumers will do anything they can to avoid having a conversation with a salesperson who uses outdated, pressure-based methods.

**The implication for salespeople:** You can no longer rely on being the information source. Your value must come from something other than product knowledge and persuasion tactics — it must come from trust, dialogue, and helping the prospect think through their own decision.

---

### 7: Ditch the Traditional Presentation


https://youtu.be/V8nRFdKXV-Q


# ## Recap: Three Forms of Communication

1. **Traditional Selling (Telling/Presenting)** — least persuasive
2. **Consultative Selling (Logical Questions)** — moderate
3. **Dialogue (NEPQs)** — most persuasive

---

## Form #1: Traditional Selling — Why Telling Fails

**The analogy:** When you tell your partner, spouse, or child to do something you want them to do, what's the typical response? They don't want to do it. They avoid it. Why? Because it doesn't directly benefit them — it primarily benefits you.

**Research confirms:** We are the least persuasive when we:
- Tell people things
- Attempt to dominate
- Posture or act tough
- Push people into doing what we want

**What traditional salespeople actually do:**
- They aren't listening for the right things
- They're too anxious to pitch their solution
- The moment they find any opening, they jump into the presentation

---

## The Consequences of Using Traditional Techniques

If you present too early in the conversation, you will **always:**
- Get objections
- Get rejected
- Have to play the numbers game
- Grind yourself into the ground for a few sales just to get by

**Why?** Because these techniques go against human behavior.

---

## The Core Problem: Focus Is on YOUR Need, Not Theirs

Traditional selling puts the salesperson's agenda first — the need to present your solution — **before finding out:**

1. Does the prospect even **have a problem**?
2. What **caused** the problem?
3. How is the problem **affecting** them?
4. Do they have a **desire to change** their situation?

When you skip these steps and jump to presenting, you're operating from your own need, not the prospect's.

---

## The Universal Test

Ask yourself: Do **you** like being pushed into buying something? Do **you** enjoy talking to someone whose only agenda is to get something from you?

What do you do when that happens? You pull away, resist, and disengage.

**Your prospects do the exact same thing.**

---

## Key Takeaway

If traditional selling techniques of telling, pitching, and presenting too early are proven to be ineffective — and they cause the mental anguish of constant rejection, objections, and grinding — the question becomes: **why are you still using them?**

The sale cannot begin with your solution. It must begin with understanding the prospect's problem, its causes, its impact, and their willingness to change.

---

### 8: NEPQ Communication


https://youtu.be/XHZk_XKquScHere's#
---

## The New Model: Engagement Is 85% of the Sale

In the new model of selling, **engagement** — not presenting — dominates the conversation. Think of it like romance: engagement is the period of getting to know someone before you commit (marry). The same applies in sales.

During the engagement phase, both you and your prospect are discovering:
- What their problems are
- What actually caused those problems
- How those problems are affecting them
- Whether what you offer can actually help

---

## The Most Important Principle

When prospects answer your skilled questions, they aren't just telling **you** their problems — they're telling **themselves**.

**The question:** Is it more persuasive when they tell themselves why they need to change, or when you tell them?

**The answer (from Behavioral Science):** It is **5x more persuasive** when prospects tell themselves they need to change, rather than the salesperson telling them.

---

## The 7-Step NEPQ Sales Structure

| Step | Question Type | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Connecting Questions** | Take the focus off you and put it on the prospect |
| 2 | **Situation Questions** | Find out their present situation |
| 3 | **Problem Awareness Questions** | Uncover what problems they have, what caused them, and how they're being affected |
| 4 | **Solution Awareness Questions** | Involve the prospect's own ideas, causing them to emotionally attach to solving their problem — with you |
| 5 | **Consequence Questions** | Help them question their current thinking and explore the consequences of NOT changing |
| 6 | **Qualifying Questions** | Confirm how important it is for them to change their situation |
| 7 | **Transition Questions** | Naturally move into presenting how your solution solves the specific problems they just told you about |

---

## New Model Time Allocation

| Activity | % of Time |
|---|---|
| **Engagement (Steps 1–7)** | **85%** |
| **Presenting Your Solution** | **10%** |
| **Closing (Committing Questions)** | **5%** |

Compare this to the old model: 10% trust, 10% needs, 50% presenting, 30% closing.

---

## Why Presenting Becomes Easy

After the engagement phase, presenting is simply **matching the answers they gave you to the correct features and benefits**. You already know exactly what to say because they've already told you:
- Their problems
- The causes
- The impact
- Their desire to change

**Old model analogy:** Traditional selling is like throwing a bucket of mud at the wall and hoping something sticks.

**New model:** You already know which mud to throw and what's going to stick — because the prospect told you through their answers.

---

## Why Closing Becomes Effortless

Only **5%** of the new model is focused on closing. Instead of high-pressure close techniques, you use **Committing Questions** that help the prospect commit and take the next step — now, not later.

This eliminates the stress, anxiety, and frustration of traditional closing because it's a **natural process**, not a battle.

---

## The Transformation: How Prospects View You

**With traditional selling:** You're seen as another pushy salesperson desperate for a commission.

**With NEPQ:** You become the **trusted authority** in your market — the advisor who helps them change their situation and solve their problems.

**The key test:** How easy would it be to sell if your prospect felt **zero sales pressure** from you — because your questions caused them to persuade themselves?

---

## Core Takeaways

1. Engagement (85%) replaces presenting (50%) as the dominant phase of the sale
2. The prospect persuading themselves is 5x more powerful than you persuading them
3. The 7-step NEPQ structure creates a logical, natural flow from discovery to commitment
4. Presenting becomes simple pattern-matching — features to stated problems
5. Closing shrinks to 5% because the sale was already made during engagement
6. NEPQ repositions you from salesperson to trusted advisor

---

## Chapter 2

### 1: Intro to the 5 Principles


https://youtu.be/bh9XLdTILDw#

---

## Purpose of This Module

To illustrate the difference between traditional selling and NEPQ by walking through a real scenario side-by-side, starting with how an average salesperson handles a warm lead.

---

## The Scenario

A lead has requested information from a website of a company that sells **digital marketing services**. The salesperson is calling to follow up.

---

## The Traditional Approach: What Goes Wrong Immediately

**The assumption:** Because the lead requested information, the salesperson assumes the prospect must be really interested in buying right now.

**The call:**

> *"Hey, this is Alex? Hey Alex, I hope you're having a good day. Anyways, my name is John Smith and you requested my company XYZ Digital Marketing to send you a packet of information on our marketing strategies that we offer to businesses like yours. Do you have two minutes to talk right now?"*

---

## Problems Identified

1. **False assumption about intent** — Requesting information ≠ ready to buy. Traditional salespeople treat every inbound lead as a hot buyer, which misreads the prospect's actual position.

2. **Generic, scripted opener** — "I hope you're having a good day" is transparent filler that signals a sales call immediately.

3. **Premature self-focus** — The salesperson leads with their company name, their product, and their agenda before learning anything about the prospect.

4. **The "two minutes" question** — Does the prospect actually believe it will only take two minutes? Of course not. They already know this is going to become a sales pitch, which triggers immediate resistance and guard-raising.

---

## Key Insight: What Is the Prospect Thinking?

At this point, the prospect is already:
- Mentally categorizing this as "another sales call"
- Preparing their exit strategy ("let me think about it," "send me an email," "not a good time")
- Guarded and skeptical

The traditional approach has already triggered **sales resistance** before the conversation has even begun.

---

## The Contrast (Preview)

The module sets up the comparison with how a salesperson trained in NEPQ would handle the exact same scenario — focusing on the prospect's world rather than the salesperson's agenda, using connecting and situation questions to create genuine dialogue instead of triggering defenses.

*(Detailed NEPQ version to follow in subsequent modules.)*

---

## Takeaway

The first 15 seconds of a sales call either **builds trust or triggers resistance**. Traditional openers — scripted pleasantries, company introductions, and time-pressure questions — immediately signal "sales call" and put the prospect on the defensive. The NEPQ approach aims to do the opposite from the very first word.

---

### 2: Principle 1 Find & Help People Solve Their


https://youtu.be/yeDQ0Svtb_A

# Lecture Notes: Principle #1 — Finding and Helping Other People Solve Their Problems

---

## The Opening Question: Why Are You in Sales?

When asked "why are you a salesperson?", most people answer with:

- "To make more money"
- "I want more flexibility with my schedule"
- "I want more freedom"
- "I want to control my own schedule"

**The problem:** All of these answers are focused on **you and your own agenda** — not on your potential customer.

---

## The Reframe

**You are not in business or sales for you. You are in business for other people.**

---

## Principle #1 of the New Model of Selling

> **The purpose of you being in sales is to find and help other people solve their problems.**

This is the foundational mindset shift. Everything in NEPQ flows from this principle.

**The test:** If your business, products, or services cannot solve your potential client's problems, is there any reason for them to do business with you? The answer is no.

---

## Application Exercise

Ask yourself:

1. Do potential clients in your specific industry have problems?
2. What are those problems? Write them down.

This isn't theoretical — you need to be able to clearly articulate the specific problems your prospects face before you can ask the right questions to uncover them in conversation.

---

## Key Takeaway

The shift from "I sell to earn money" to "I sell to solve people's problems" isn't just a feel-good reframe — it fundamentally changes how you open conversations, what questions you ask, how you listen, and when you present your solution. When your focus is genuinely on the prospect's problems, sales pressure disappears because you're no longer pushing an agenda — you're helping someone find a resolution.

---

### 3: Principle 2 Ask Right Questions at Right Time


https://youtu.be/cKey0aSTyJIHere's
# Lecture Notes: Principle #2 — Ask the Right Questions at the Right Time

---

## The True Essence of Selling

Contrary to what you've been taught, selling is **not** about:
- Convincing
- Persuading
- Manipulating
- Pushing someone into doing what you want

That's what average salespeople do — and it's what creates the cycle of rejection and objections.

---

## The Objection Cycle: A Self-Created Problem

When you tell people about you, your company, your products, and your solutions **too early** in the conversation, you trigger sales pressure and resistance, which leads to objections like:

- "Your product doesn't do this"
- "This is too expensive"
- "I read a negative review online"
- "What about this? It doesn't do that"

**The irony:** Traditional techniques cause the objections in the first place. Then you have to learn objection handling techniques and closing techniques to overcome what **you yourself created** by the way you communicated.

The cycle looks like this:

> Traditional techniques → Sales pressure → Objections → Rebuttals → More resistance → Stats and facts to prove your point → Rarely works → Grind and hustle

**The question:** Why would anyone want to do all that when you don't have to?

---

## What NEPQ Questions Actually Are

NEPQ questions are **not** questions designed to get people to say what you want them to say.

They are questions intended to bring out:
- People's **inner and external truths**
- Their **feelings and emotions**
- Their own story — told by them, about themselves, to you

**The critical insight:** Who knows the most about your potential customer — them or you? They do. So let them talk.

---

## The Core Principle

> **Don't try to persuade them. Ask questions to get them to persuade themselves.**

---

## How Self-Persuasion Works

**Your prospect** has:
- Their own history
- Knowledge of their own needs
- All the answers you need to make the sale

**You** have:
- Knowledge of which problems your solution solves
- The ability to recognize whether your solution fits this specific customer
- The skill to explain it in a way that makes 100% sense to them

**All you need are the right questions — and knowing when to ask them.**

---

## What Skilled Questions Eliminate

When you ask the right questions at the right time, you eliminate the need for:
- Presenting too early
- Telling your story
- Closing techniques
- Objection handling techniques

**Who eliminates the objections and concerns?** The prospect does — because they're the ones talking about themselves and revealing their problems. And more importantly, they're revealing those problems not just to you, but **to themselves**.

---

## Key Takeaway

The right questions, asked at the right time, replace the entire traditional sales toolkit — presenting, pitching, closing, and objection handling. The prospect does the heavy lifting because they're processing their own situation out loud. Your job is simply to guide that process with skilled, well-timed questions, then match your solution to what they've already told you they need.

---

### 4: Principle 3 Listening to What Your ProspectMean

https://youtu.be/7afpVLcMark

\# Lecture Notes: Principle #3 — Listening to What Your Prospect Means, Not Just What They Say

---

## The Power of Listening in Sales

Listening is not passive — it's a **powerful tool** in the NEPQ toolkit, equal in importance to asking the right questions.

**What skilled listening does:**

-   Takes the pressure off the conversation
    
-   Enables you to present effectively (because you actually heard what matters)
    
-   Makes your prospect feel understood
    
-   Draws potential customers to you like a magnet
    

---

## Open Listening: The Definition

**Open listening** means:

-   Listening **without judging**
    
-   Focusing entirely on the **other person**
    
-   Putting aside **your agenda** (making the sale)
    

This single practice will **completely differentiate you** from every other salesperson in your prospect's eyes.

---

## The Reality: Salespeople Don't Listen

Based on training experience, most salespeople don't truly listen to their prospects at all.

**An ironic pattern:** We tend to listen the least to the people we're closest to — and in sales, we often listen least to the very people we're trying to help.

**The question posed:** How much time do you think most salespeople actually spend listening to their prospects?

*(The implication: very little — because they're too focused on their own agenda, waiting for an opening to pitch.)*

---

## The Connection to NEPQ

Listening and questioning work together as a system:

-   **Questions** get prospects to reveal their problems, emotions, and truths
    
-   **Listening** ensures you actually capture what they're saying — and what they **mean**
    
-   Without real listening, even the best questions are wasted because you miss the deeper meaning behind the answers
    

---

## Key Takeaway

Most salespeople are mentally rehearsing their pitch while the prospect is talking. Open listening — genuinely focusing on the other person without judgment or agenda — is rare enough in sales that simply doing it sets you apart. When prospects feel truly heard, trust builds naturally, resistance drops, and the path to presenting your solution becomes clear because you actually understood what they need.

---

### 5: 5 Active Listening Skills to Increase Sales P.1

# Lecture Notes: 5 Active Listening Skills to Increase Your Sales (Part 1)

---

## Active Listening Skill #1: Be Aware (Be Present)

---

### The Concept Through Everyday Experiences

Think about times when someone wasn't fully present while you were talking:

**Scenario 1:** You're having a conversation with a friend at a restaurant, and mid-sentence they look away to watch a waiter or waitress walk by.

**Scenario 2:** You're having a deep conversation with someone, they receive a text message, and while you're still sharing your issues they start reading the text.

**Scenario 3:** You're telling a story to a group of friends, and someone breaks in with "Oh my gosh, the same thing happened to me — let me tell you about it."

---

### How Did Those Experiences Make You Feel?

- Annoyed
- Frustrated
- Disrespected
- Invalidated — like what you were saying didn't matter

**What did the other person communicate through their actions?** That they weren't really present in the conversation.

---

### The Self-Assessment

Now turn it on yourself:

- Have you ever done any of these things when you were with a potential customer?
- Were you ever **not present** in a conversation with a prospect?

If you've been distracted, mentally preparing your pitch, checking your phone, or waiting for your turn to talk — your prospect felt exactly the same way you did in those scenarios above.

---

### Key Takeaway

**Being aware** means being fully present in the conversation — not thinking about your next question, your pitch, your commission, or the text that just came in. When you're not present, your prospect feels it immediately, and it communicates that you don't genuinely care about what they're saying. Awareness is the foundation all other listening skills are built on — without it, nothing else works.

---

### 6: 5 Active Listening Skills to Increase Sales P.2

# Lecture Notes: The Power of Silence & Understanding in Sales Conversations

---

## The Problem: Filling Silence

Most salespeople, when faced with silence after asking a question, do one of two things:
- Ask another question
- Answer the question themselves

**Why is this a problem?**
- You asked a question but won't let the prospect answer it
- The prospect changes the subject, asks a question back, and the conversation goes off track
- Sales conversations break down
- It makes your potential customer feel **invalidated**

**Root cause:** Salespeople are afraid of losing control of the conversation. But the truth is the exact opposite — **being quiet puts you in control.**

---

## Why Silence = Control

When you let go of the attachment to making the sale, you become more open to your prospect's needs and feelings. When you become more open, **your prospect becomes more open too** — and they'll tell you the truth.

**Ditch the old cliche: "Buyers are liars."** This is old-school thinking from salespeople who don't know how to sell to today's consumers. People lie to salespeople because they don't trust them. Remove that mindset.

**The Golden Rule of Silence:**
- Ask a question → Be silent → Be comfortable with that silence
- Being quiet is NOT a trick or closing technique — it's simply being **respectful and courteous**
- It allows your prospect to reflect and provide genuine answers

---

## The Onion Analogy

The first answers your prospect gives are just the **first layer of the onion.** As you ask deeper questions:
- Answers become more truthful
- Answers become more emotional
- You peel off more layers

**Key rules:**
1. Allow your prospect to answer the questions you ask
2. Never interrupt to answer with your own questions
3. Never change the subject

**Silence is golden.**

---

## Skill #5: Be Understanding

**The Debate Trap:**
Think back to a time someone said something you completely disagreed with. You debated, you argued, you used "Yes, but..." responses to try persuading them. How many of those debates did you actually win? Did the other person suddenly change their view to yours? **Probably not.**

**The Alternative Approach:**
Instead of debating, what if you:
- Actually listened to the other person
- Asked deeper questions to understand where they were coming from and why they felt that way
- Did all of this **without judgment** and without injecting your own personal interpretation

**What would have happened:**
1. You gain more understanding of their perspective
2. The other person may subconsciously question their own thinking as they hear themselves answer your skilled questions
3. They might reconsider their point of view
4. They become **much more open** to listening to you

---

## Stephen Covey's Principle

> *"First seek to understand, and then be understood."*
> — Stephen Covey, *The 7 Habits of Highly Effective People*

**Key distinction:** Listening to and accepting another person's point of view does NOT mean you have to change your own beliefs. You don't have to agree with someone to listen to them.

---

## The Transformation

When you suspend your assumptions about how you view the world:
- Prospects will **want** to listen to you
- You'll seem knowledgeable, accepting, and interesting
- You can literally **listen your prospects into changing** by staying open with a desire to understand

**If you want your prospects to change, work on changing yourself first.**

You'll start hearing things in sales conversations that were always there but you never noticed — because you weren't listening.

**The result:** Greater understanding of people, and they will have greater understanding and respect for you. **When you listen and accept others, they will always listen and accept you.**

---

## Key Takeaways

1. **Silence after a question is powerful** — resist the urge to fill it
2. **Silence = control**, not weakness
3. **First answers are surface-level** — go deeper with follow-up questions
4. **Never debate or argue** — seek to understand first
5. **Listening and accepting ≠ agreeing** — you can understand without changing your beliefs
6. **Change yourself first** — your openness will naturally influence your prospects

---

### 7: 5 Active Listening Skills to Increase Sales P.3


https://youtu.be/_fAgnVb0WrQHere's 
# Lecture Notes: Keys to Being Understanding & What Listening Is Not

---

## Keys to Being Understanding

*Note: These principles apply not just to sales, but to all areas of your life.*

---

### Key #1: Accept Without Interjecting Your Assumptions

- Be very careful about making assumptions about your prospects
- When you assume what potential customers mean by what they're telling you, you only get **superficial, surface-level answers** — leading to average results
- **Always ask them what they mean** — you will be shocked at how differently people think from you
- Too many sales are lost because salespeople assume they understand the customer's problems when they really don't
  - They don't know how deep those problems go
  - They don't know how those problems are truly affecting the customer
- **Why does this happen?** They fail to ask and fail to listen. They assume they know because they've heard similar objections from other prospects in the past.

---

### Key #2: Accept Without Judging or Interjecting Your Interpretation

- Don't judge others by your own reality
- When you judge, you're not changing the other person at all — you're only demonstrating your need to criticize and undermine them
- **Let go of your need to judge and you will be free**

**Practical exercise:** Next time someone doesn't move when the light turns green, don't honk. Just sit there. Give them room. You'll experience a massive difference in your happiness, health, and stress levels. You don't really know why they didn't move — maybe their child was crying in the back seat, maybe they just had a death in the family.

---

### Key #3: Don't Let Your Beliefs, Experiences, and Ego Get in the Way

- Don't interpret what the other person is saying through your own lens
- **Avoid phrases like:**
  - "That must have been awful for you"
  - "I know how you feel"
- Chances are it might NOT have been awful for them, and you probably DON'T know how they feel — that's just your interpretation
- **Instead, ask:** *"How did that make you feel?"*

**The Therapist Principle:** The world's best therapists (earning hundreds of dollars per hour) know that giving patients answers doesn't have the same persuasive effect as when patients provide answers themselves through the therapist's skilled questions. When patients hear themselves talk, they think about their words and **automatically own their statements.**

**It's the same thing in sales.** Let the prospect arrive at their own conclusions through your skilled questioning.

---

## What Listening Is NOT

Listening is sometimes confused with recognizing connections between the customer's thoughts and your own. This is a critical mistake.

**The 90/100 Rule:**
Even if you know 90% of the customer's story, you need to hear **100%** of it. What you already know cannot be subtracted from what you need to hear from your prospect.

---

### Example: The Wrong Way

**Prospect says:**
*"You know Sally, we're going through a major restructuring of the entire company right now. Our CEO just retired, a lot of things are up in the air, and now our VP of Sales is taking over this department."*

**Average salesperson responds:**
*"I already knew that — I read that in the Wall Street Journal yesterday."*

**What the prospect is actually thinking:**
- NOT: *"Wow, this salesperson is so smart! I don't have to tell them much, we can save time."*
- MORE LIKELY: *"What does this outsider possibly know about what's going on inside MY company? This restructure affects ME, not them."*

---

## Key Takeaways

1. **Never assume** — always ask what they mean, even if you think you already know
2. **Never judge** — suspend your reality and let the prospect share theirs
3. **Never interpret through your ego** — ask how THEY feel rather than projecting how you think they feel
4. **Let prospects provide their own answers** — ownership of statements creates persuasion (the therapist principle)
5. **Listen to 100%** of what the prospect tells you, even if you think you already know most of it
6. **Never one-up your prospect** — showing off your knowledge shuts down their openness

---

### 8: Principle 4 Use Feedback & Commitment Formula


https://youtu.be/IL9T0LyJskU
# Lecture Notes: Principle #4 — The Feedback & Commitment Formula to Close

---

## Overview

This principle is **twofold**: Feedback + Commitment. Together they form a natural, trust-based closing process.

---

## Part 1: Feedback (Restating & Summarizing)

Feedback in this context means **restating and summarizing** what your potential customer told you about:
- Their unique situation
- Their problems and challenges

**When does this happen?**
- At the end of the sales conversation
- After you've gone through the discovery/engagement process
- When you're presenting your solution

**Why does it work?**
- You are properly positioned because of the trust you've built
- You demonstrate a **complete understanding** of their situation
- You're using THEIR words, not yours

**Remember:** Presenting is only **5% of the NEQ process** (the new model).

---

## The Formula: Present + Qualify + Commit

### Step 1: Present — Summarize Their Problems

Use this framework:

> *"So [name], you know how you said..."*
> → Repeat back the problem they told you
>
> *"...and..."*
> → Repeat back an important **logical** problem
>
> *"...and because of this, it's making you feel..."*
> → Repeat back an important **emotional** problem they shared

**Example (Financial Services):**

*"So John, you know how you said you've been investing with this brokerage company for the last three years, but you don't feel like they're really giving you the return you wanted... and because of that, it's causing you to feel — I think you mentioned — a little bit of worry, because you need to retire soon."*

### Step 2: Explain Your Solution Briefly

> *"Well, what I do is..."*
> → Fill in what you do
>
> *"...and what that means to you is..."*
> → Describe the **benefits** (not just features) that satisfy the **personal/emotional side** of their problem

---

### Step 3: Qualify — Ask a Qualifying Question

Use this phrasing with **pause tonality** (not robotic):

> *"Does that feel... as though it might be... maybe what you're looking for?"*

**Critical — Tonality matters:**
- DON'T say it flat/scripted: *"John, does that feel like it might be what you're looking for?"*
- DO use natural pauses and softer tonality — it should feel conversational, not rehearsed

---

### Step 4: Probe — Ask Them WHY

After they answer (e.g., "Yeah, I feel like it is"), **probe deeper:**

> *"Well hold on... why do you feel like it is, though?"*

**Key tonality:** Use gentle skepticism — as if you're genuinely curious, slightly questioning. NOT aggressive or interrogating.

**Why this matters:**
- When you ask them why your solution fits, **they tell themselves** why it works
- Self-persuasion is far more powerful than you persuading them

> **Which is more persuasive?**
> - You telling them why it's what they're looking for? ❌
> - Them telling THEMSELVES it's what they're looking for? ✅

**Always ask them why they feel your solution is what they're looking for.** When they answer, they're not just telling you — they're convincing themselves.

---

## Key Takeaways

1. **Feedback = summarizing their situation** using their own words (logical + emotional problems)
2. **Present your solution briefly** — focus on benefits tied to their personal/emotional needs
3. **Qualify with soft tonality** — "Does that feel like it might be what you're looking for?"
4. **Probe with gentle skepticism** — "Why do you feel like it is, though?"
5. **Self-persuasion is the goal** — the most powerful close is when the prospect convinces themselves
6. **Tonality is everything** — same words said differently produce completely different results

---

### 9: The Most Important Committing Questions


https://youtu.be/gz76Aj-K8do
# Lecture Notes: The Most Important Committing Questions You'll Ever Learn

## Principle: Committing (Closing) Through Neutral Language

---

## The First Committing Question

At this point in the conversation, you've completed your engagement and presentation. Now you make a suggestion and ask final committing questions.

**The core question:**
> "Amy, do you feel like this... could be the answer for you?"

### Critical Delivery Elements

**Tonality & Pausing:** Notice the deliberate pause before "could be the answer for you." You must pause — don't rush through it. If you say it without pausing, you sound scripted and robotic, which triggers pushback.

**Neutral Language ("Could Be"):** This is what separates top salespeople from everyone else. By saying "could be," you remain neutral — you're not assuming the sale.

**Wrong way (assumptive — triggers resistance):**
- "John, do you think this IS really the answer for you?"
- "John, I KNOW this is the answer for you."

**Right way (neutral — invites agreement):**
- "Does this sound like it could be the answer for you?"
- "Does this look like it could be what you're looking for?"

**Key Insight:** Any NEPQ salesperson who's mastered the new model always uses neutral language. Average salespeople assume the sale — and in today's environment, that's why they lose sales they could be making.

---

## The Probing Follow-Up: "Why Though?"

When they say "Yeah, I do" — **always ask why.**

> "Well, hold on... why do you feel like it is though?"

This is a critical NEPQ probing question. It pulls out their emotion and gets the prospect to **tell themselves** why they want to do business with you.

**Why this matters:** When people tell themselves why they want your solution, that is infinitely more persuasive than YOU telling them they should buy. They own it as their future.

---

## The Second Committing Question

> "Sally, do you feel like this... can get you where you're wanting to go?"

Adapt this to your product/service scenario:

- "Do you feel like this is what you're wanting?"
- "Do you feel like this can give you what you've been wanting to do?"

Again, when they say yes — ask the probing follow-up:
> "Hold on... why do you feel like it would?"

They fight back with their own reasons: "Well, it would because of this, and because of that..." — they're selling themselves.

---

## Industry-Specific Examples

| Industry | Committing Question |
|---|---|
| **Coaching / Network Marketing / Recruiting** | "Do you feel like this is something you can do to get you where you're wanting to go in your life?" |
| **Financial Services** | "Do you feel like this is something you can do to get you where you're wanting to go with your investment portfolio?" |
| **Insurance (Life)** | "Do you feel like this would give you something that would give you more peace of mind to make sure they were covered in case something did happen to you?" |
| **Real Estate** | "Do you feel like this would be the home for you and your family?" |

**In every case**, when they say yes, follow up with: *"Why do you feel like it would though?"*

---

## Transitioning to the Commitment / Next Steps

Once the probing questions are done and they've verbalized their own reasons, transition naturally:

> "You know, John, I don't really have anything else to go over with you. It looks like we've covered the basis of what you're looking for. Really, the next step would be — if it's appropriate — is we would [explain next steps and how to get started]."

Then ask one of these closing questions **with a deliberate pause:**

- "Would that be appropriate... or how would you like to proceed from here?"
- "Would you feel comfortable with that?"

**Do NOT rush this.** If you say it without pausing, you sound like a robot and get pushback.

**Black Belt Question (use only when experienced):**
> "Where do you think we should go from here?"

⚠️ Only use this when you're fully confident handling any response. If you don't know how to respond to what they say, don't use it yet.

---

## The Calendar Statement Commitment

This is used when asking for commitment at a specific point in time. At this stage, prior agreements are in place, the prospect feels momentum, and they see you as the trusted authority. Committing is just the logical next step.

> "Jessica, in order for us to do this for you... would it be appropriate for us to get out our calendars and schedule the next steps?"

**Key phrase: "In order to do this FOR YOU"** — you're carrying out the customer's wishes. Committing becomes the last of many logical conclusions in the conversation.

**What committing really means:**
1. You've demonstrated understanding of the customer's problems
2. You've fit the correct solution to solve those problems
3. You are putting the proposed solution into effect

There are no fancy tricks, no manipulative techniques. Be **detached** from your expectations of making the sale. Focus on seeing if there's a sale to be made in the first place. Avoid any sales pressure — at this point they're bought in.

---

## Conditional Decision Questions

You can also ask for a conditional decision:

- "Jessica, if you were to go ahead... can I ask when would you...?"
- "John, is this something you're looking for now, or when do you want to [fill in their solution]?"

### Industry-Specific Conditional Examples

| Scenario | Question |
|---|---|
| Prospect wants to make more money (franchise/recruiting) | "Is this something you're looking for now, or when do you want to find a business you can start making more money?" |
| Prospect concerned about bad neighborhood (real estate) | "Is this something you're looking for now, or when do you want to find a new home so that you can get your kids in a better neighborhood?" |

**Principle:** Plug in what THEY told you their problem was. Use their own words.

---

## Fixing Dates & Managing Next Steps

### Four Types of Next Step Actions:
1. Actions the potential customer offers to take
2. Actions the potential customer wants you to take
3. Actions you offer to take
4. Actions you want the potential customer to take

### Example: Truck Maintenance Program

The full dialogue demonstrates how to:
- Use the calendar statement to set a target date
- Ask "What's the first step you need to take on your end?"
- Offer your own next steps ("With your permission, tomorrow morning I can scan and email you a form...")
- Set specific deadlines and confirm them
- Always ask: **"Is there anything you'd like to address with me at this point?"**

⚠️ **Critical:** If the prospect has concerns and doesn't voice them now, you could be toast when they talk to their legal team, spouse, or manager. Surface concerns NOW so you can address them.

---

## Key Language Substitutions

| ❌ Never Say | ✅ Always Say |
|---|---|
| Contract | Agreement |
| Sign | Authorize |

Customers don't like "signing contracts" — but they do like "authorizing agreements." The neutral language removes negative connotations.

---

## Summary of Key Principles

1. **Use neutral language** — "could be," not "is" or "I know"
2. **Pause deliberately** — tonality and pausing have huge psychological impact
3. **Always ask "why though?"** — get the prospect to sell themselves
4. **Detach from the outcome** — focus on whether there's a sale to be made, not on making one
5. **Use calendar statements** — make commitment a logical next step, not a pressure move
6. **Plug in their words** — use what they told you about their problems in your committing questions
7. **Surface all concerns** — ask if there's anything else they want to address before you part ways
8. **Replace negative terms** — agreement not contract, authorize not sign
9. **No manipulation needed** — when done right, committing is simply the natural conclusion of a well-conducted conversation

---

### 10: Principle 5 Eliminate Sales Pressure


https://youtu.be/sts4TYLkzIg
# Lecture Notes: Principle #5 — Eliminate Sales Pressure & Resistance

---

## Core Principle: Detach from the Expectation of Making the Sale

The foundational mindset shift: **Focus on whether or not there's a sale to be made in the first place** — not on making the sale.

When you detach from the expectation of making the sale, two things happen automatically:
1. You take the pressure out of your sales conversations
2. Your potential customers feel comfortable telling you the **truth** about their needs — which is what's actually required for them to move forward with your solution

---

## How Sales Pressure Gets Triggered

**The mechanism is simple:** When the language you use is **not trust-based or neutral**, you immediately trigger sales pressure → which leads to rejection.

When you use **neutral language** properly, you don't trigger rejection. The correct language is pressure-free, and your potential client will feel that from you.

**Imagine how selling would be if your potential clients didn't feel any sales pressure from you.**

---

## Example: Setting a Follow-Up Appointment

### What most salespeople have been trained to say:

> "John, when can we get together for another call? Do you want to talk again Thursday afternoon or Friday morning?"

This is the classic **option close** — and here's the problem:

**You're assuming they even want to have another conversation.**

When you assume, you become just another salesperson in their mind who's trying to sell them something. You sound like every other salesperson who calls them every day. There is **nothing different** about you in their minds — and that is a dangerous place to be.

---

## The Problem with Traditional Selling

Traditional selling has conditioned us to focus on **making the sale now** — and if we don't, we feel like we're not selling right.

But what happens when you try to persuade them to move things forward on the first call **when they're not ready for that?**

You lose the deal entirely. Trust is broken. The relationship is over before it started.

---

## Key Takeaways

1. **Detachment = freedom from pressure.** When you stop focusing on closing, the prospect relaxes and opens up.
2. **Neutral language prevents rejection.** Trust-based, non-assumptive phrasing keeps the conversation safe for the prospect.
3. **Stop using the option close for follow-ups.** Assuming they want another meeting makes you sound like every other salesperson.
4. **Differentiation comes from how you make them feel.** If prospects feel zero pressure from you, you stand apart from everyone else calling them.
5. **Pushing when they're not ready destroys the deal.** Trying to force forward momentum on the first call when they're not there yet costs you the entire opportunity.

---

## Connection to Previous Principles

This principle reinforces everything from the committing questions (Principle 4):
- Use **"could be"** not **"is"** — neutral, not assumptive
- **Pause deliberately** — don't rush or pressure
- Ask **"why though?"** — let them sell themselves
- Use **calendar statements** that frame next steps as carrying out THEIR wishes, not yours
- Replace high-pressure terms (contract → agreement, sign → authorize)

**The thread running through all of NEPQ:** The prospect should never feel like they're being sold to. They should feel like they're being helped to make the right decision for themselves.

---

### 11: Sales Myths vs Sales Reality


https://youtu.be/OYySuNuba0k
# Lecture Notes: Sales Myths vs. Sales Reality

---

## Sales Myth #1: "Selling Is Just a Numbers Game"

### The Origin of this Myth

Somewhere, decades ago, a sales manager on some sales floor told his team: *"Hey, selling is just a numbers game"* — in order to make them feel better about facing constant rejection 95% of the time.

### When It's True (and Why That's a Problem)

Selling IS a numbers game — **if all you use are traditional selling techniques.** You can:
- Cold call hundreds of people a day with "Hi, my name is ___, I'm with ___, we do ___"
- Make a few sales and eke out a living
- Chase prospects down until they listen just long enough to come up with an excuse to get rid of you

### The Reality in the Post-Trust Era

With trust at its lowest point ever, it's **no longer about quantity — it's about quality.**

What actually determines your success:
- The **quality of your conversations**
- Your ability to **bring out their emotions** by going deeper with your questions
- How good you are at **creating trust**
- How good you are at **detaching yourself** from the expectation of making the sale

### Your Identity Shift

You are a **problem finder and problem solver.** This allows you to become more open to them. You learn:
- What their problems are
- What caused the problems
- How the problems are affecting them
- Whether your solution can actually help them

### The Magnetic Effect

When you have these conversations in a **calm, conversational voice** — as opposed to the typical over-enthusiastic sales pitch voice — you automatically attract potential customers to you like a magnet.

**Why?** Because for the first time in a long time, someone is genuinely interested in **them** and what they're looking for. This is the opposite of the typical salesperson who's just trying to stuff their agenda down the prospect's throat.

**The result:** They feel your intent, they start to lean on you, and you become their **trusted advisor** — the authority in their minds.

---

## Sales Myth #2: "Rejection Is Just Part of Selling"

### The Traditional Narrative

You've been told: rejection is part of selling, expect it, get over it, grow thick skin. You may have even learned how to do that.

**This is complete nonsense.**

### The Real Question to Ask Yourself

If you're getting anxious about meeting prospects, cold calling, or calling leads to the point you can't talk to them — **something is wrong.** Consider:

- Do you need to get pumped up before every meeting or call?
- Do you drag your feet before making calls?
- Have you ever considered that **it could be the way you're communicating** that's causing the problem?

**What if your prospects reject you because YOU'RE the one triggering the rejection by the way you communicate with them?**

### Two Types of Rejection

| Type | What It Looks Like | How It Feels |
|---|---|---|
| **Type 1: Flat-out rejection** | "I'm not interested." / "We already have that." | Personal, painful — the sting |
| **Type 2: Genuine mismatch** | Prospect evaluates your solution and determines it doesn't solve their needs | Disappointing but not personal |

Type 2 is part of business — not every solution fits every problem.

Type 1, however, is **largely caused by how you're communicating.** When you master NEPQ, you learn how to **diffuse the cause of rejection** — not just cope with it. That's a big difference.

### The Key Insight

You can actually **eliminate** rejection — not through mind games, hypnosis, mental tricks, or pumping yourself up with personal development content. You eliminate it by **removing the triggers** in your communication that cause prospects to reject you in the first place.

---

## Sales Myth #3: "You've Got to Be Enthusiastic — If the Customer Sees You're Excited, They'll Get Excited Too"

### Why This Is Wrong

Never assume that your excitement about what you sell will just rub off on your clients. Their motivation for buying or making a change **varies from person to person.**

### What Happens When You Approach with Too Much External Enthusiasm

**Reaction 1 — Withdrawal:**
- They get overwhelmed
- They say things like: "Oh, this sounds good — leave me some information" or "This is wonderful, let me think it over and I'll call you back"
- Then they disappear — don't return calls, texts, or emails — they go **MIA (Missing in Action)**

**Reaction 2 — Defensiveness:**
- They throw out objections
- They flat-out reject you and what you're offering

### The Takeaway

Over-enthusiasm is a **sales pressure trigger.** It signals to the prospect that you're a salesperson with an agenda, not a trusted advisor genuinely interested in their situation. The calm, conversational approach always outperforms the hyped-up pitch.

---

## Summary: Myths vs. Reality

| Myth | Reality |
|---|---|
| Selling is a numbers game | It's about the **quality** of conversations, not quantity of calls |
| Rejection is just part of selling | Rejection is largely **triggered by your communication** — and can be eliminated |
| Enthusiasm sells | Over-enthusiasm **overwhelms or triggers defensiveness** — calm conversation attracts |

**The common thread:** All three myths come from traditional selling. All three are eliminated when you adopt the NEPQ methodology — neutral language, trust-based conversations, genuine interest in the prospect's problems, and detachment from the outcome.

---

### 12: 5 Principles Summary


https://youtu.be/7BbJ8aIK5XQ

# Lecture Notes: The 5 Principles of NEPQ — Summary

---

## What Is NEPQ?

**NEPQ = Neuro-Emotional Persuasion Questions**

When you use these questions, you:
1. **Find out fast** whether there's a sale to be made
2. **Build trust** with the prospect
3. **Allow prospects to persuade themselves**
4. **Remove stress, pressure, and resistance** from the sales equation

---

## The 5 Stages of the NEPQ Sales Process

| Stage | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **1. Connecting** | Build initial rapport and differentiate yourself from every other salesperson |
| **2. Engagement** | Ask skilled questions to uncover problems, causes, and emotional impact |
| **3. Transitional** | Bridge from problem discovery to solution presentation |
| **4. Presentation** | Fit your solution to their specific problems using what they've told you |
| **5. Commitment** | Guide the prospect to the logical next step — pressure-free |

Each stage has **specific NEPQ questions** designed to move the conversation forward naturally, without triggering sales resistance.

---

## Quick Recap: The 5 Principles Covered So Far

1. **Ask questions instead of presenting** — Skilled questions allow the prospect to discover their own need for your solution
2. **Engage through emotional discovery** — Go deeper into problems, causes, and impact to bring out real buying motivation
3. **Use neutral language throughout** — "Could be," not "is." Agreements, not contracts. Calm tone, not over-enthusiasm.
4. **Commit through logical conclusion** — When done right, committing is simply the last in a series of logical steps the prospect has already agreed to
5. **Eliminate sales pressure & resistance** — Detach from the outcome, stop triggering rejection, and become the trusted advisor they lean on

---

## What's Next

The deep dive into each stage — learning the **exact questions** to ask at every point in the sales conversation, from the first connection through to commitment.

---

## Chapter 3

### 1: What are Connecting Questions and Why Use Them


https://youtu.be/fh5k4RfC2Po
# Lecture Notes: What Are Connecting Questions, and Why Use Them?

---

## The Connecting Stage — Overview

The connecting stage is the **very first stage** of the NEPQ sales process. It sets the tone for everything that follows.

---

## Why the First Words Matter

In any conversation with a prospect, the **first words you say** — and more importantly, the **first questions you ask** — can either make you or break you.

**Two possible outcomes from your opening:**

| Outcome | What Happens |
|---|---|
| **Done right** | People are drawn to you and become open to what you're selling |
| **Done wrong** | You trigger sales resistance → objections → rejection |

---

## What Are Connecting Questions?

Connecting questions are the key to:
1. **Creating a favorable first impression**
2. **Focusing on your potential customer** (not on you or your product)

---

## The Emotional Reality of First Contact

Both you AND your potential customer experience strong emotions at the start of a sales conversation. Recognizing and managing these emotions is what the connecting stage is designed to address.

**For the salesperson:** Anxiety, fear of rejection, pressure to perform
**For the prospect:** Guard goes up, skepticism, assumption they're about to be pitched

The connecting questions are specifically designed to **neutralize these emotions on both sides** — creating an environment where genuine conversation can happen.

---

## Key Takeaway

Everything starts here. If you get the connecting stage wrong, it doesn't matter how good your engagement questions, presentation, or commitment process is. The prospect has already mentally checked out. Connecting questions are your gateway to earning the right to ask deeper questions later in the conversation.

---
